Ninety-two years ago, on March 7, 1921, Artvin and our surrounding people's struggle to exist against the Russians and Armenians were crowned with the diplomatic and military achievements of the new Turkish government, and the people of Artvin were once again and forever free. The Berlin Agreement, signed on 13 July 1873, was awarded to the Russians as compensation for the war of Artvin, and that was the beginning of the 40 years of captivity that our people called the black days. In November 1914, the Melo border battalion, under the command of Captain Ismail Hakk Bey, defeated the forces of the Tsarate Russian army around Artvin, and the Russians had to leave Artvin. The number of arrests is increasing daily, and many of the students who are being sued are facing severe penalties. The concept of prison students is becoming increasingly important in social and political areas and leads to reactions in society. The most painful problem with prison students is that their education lives are interrupted or completely over. Even if a student is a prisoner, it's a life-changing event. However, there are numerous reports that neither prison nor university administrations have been sensitive about it. The matter is often carried out in letters sent to me by students in prison. The news and letters say that university administrations do not take students for safety reasons, and prison administrations demand road and food allowance from imprisoned students. In this case, students' education has been virtually interrupted or completely terminated. Students who have been arrested, especially students who teach in foreign languages, also have problems providing grades and preparing for their exams. The lessons and notes sent to prisons are not delivered to students, nor are they forwarded to them. Reading and notes in foreign languages are delivered to the person sent months or even years later, which makes it impossible for the student to prepare for his exams. When many students are detained, arrested or sued, university administrations are conducting disciplinary investigations against students and sentences such as suspension and expulsion of students before the verdict is finalised. In this case, the purity of the law is being punished in advance, inadvertently. The vast majority of these sentences are being applied to actions carried out due to the use of constitutional rights and right to march. tbmm-2013-03-07sit04spe0181par0040	Mr. President, I have made a commitment to the amendment motion on article 9 of Power Market Act 426, with respect to the General Assembly. Before I begin my speech, I too celebrate the 8th of March World Laboring Women's Day, and pay my respects. Dear lawmakers, energy -- one of the most important entries in economic and social development -- has been occupying the agenda of all the countries of the world from the 70s to the present day, and wars are taking place. In this context, energy is an essential component of development programmes, with its key role in economic growth. Energy policies in particular form an integrated part of sustainable development plans in developing countries. The use of electricity energy, a basic input of all kinds of economic activity, has become an inescapable element of social life, as energy and electricity are constantly increasing depending on developing technology today. Electric energy consumption is one of the most important indicators of economic and social development. The production or consumption of electricity per capita in one country is important to reflect the standard of life in that country. As of April 2012, 45 per cent of Turkey's total electricity production is covered by natural gas, 28 per cent by coal, 23 per cent by hydroelectric sources and 2.1 per cent by wind energy. Natural gas still has the highest share in our production of electrical energy. In Turkey, which is a net importer country in terms of energy sources, 73 per cent of the energy supply is covered by imports. As expressed in the previous draft law as the Peace and Democracy Party, the need for the consumer to organise sufficient, cheap and quality electricity has unfortunately been ignored in this bill. The whole point is that because it's market satisfaction, we're taking it normally, but we're not going to give up on the fact that citizens need cheap and quality electricity. The Constitution must meet the obligations of being a social state that exists in our constitution as a government executive, providing our citizens with basic means of living, such as water and electricity. With the government's responsibilities in the open, there's a reverse process in our country, energy prices are increasing in every way. The system in the electric market works as a non-expression of this cheapness when electricity energy is cheaply obtained. It also means that companies in the industry make more profit. Therefore, what needs to be done is to ensure that electricity is offered to consumers at a low price, as well as the low cost criteria. Mr. President, dear lawmakers, this bill is not a public law, but a bill that will help private firms to continue their construction to the HES firms, whose license has been revoked or stopped. The 9th article of the bill does not satisfy the uncertainty about how the losses will be met, but it keeps the distribution companies' area very high. As it is known, the previous law forbids production companies to become shareholders in order to establish control over distribution companies. Article 9 of the bill removes the restrictions, but prevents distribution companies from becoming direct or indirect shareholders in any other corporate person operating in the electricity market. I hereby await the acceptance of our amendment proposal, and salute the General Assembly. Thank you.

In the coming years, predictions in this area show that it will again be 7 percent and above. While Turkey's energy demand is increasing, I must point out that we have also imported 80 per cent of this demand. Has the government responded to this growing demand? Has it increased domestic production and improved in terms of supply security? No. On the contrary, there's a total decline in domestic production. The amount of domestic production, which was 28 per cent in 2003, was reduced to 27 per cent, as of 2012. We know that Turkey, which paid $55 billion in energy imports, heavily oil and gas, in 2011, reached $62 billion at the end of 2012. I think we can describe the importance of this figure in a more specific way: this number is more than one third of the foreign trade deficit. We're 94% dependent on oil, 97 percent on natural gas. What's our status on the production of electrical energy? 43 per cent of electricity produced in Turkey is derived from natural gas, 24 per cent from hydropower plants, 20 per cent from domestic coal, 7 per cent from imported coal, 2 per cent from wind, 4 per cent from liquid fuel. tbmm-2013-03-07sit06spe0221par0188	Durdu Özbolat, Alderman of Kahramanmarash.
tbmm-2013-03-07sit06spe0223par0190	Mr. President, I have made a promise regarding Article 13 of Law 426. I salute the General Assembly. The 13th article, which we discussed, was especially needed to regulate the problems of organised industrial areas. Organised industrial areas are the most important industrial and trade areas in our country, the most intensive of exports and production in Turkey. Turkey consumes 20 per cent of electricity in organised industrial areas. This figure is the largest part of Turkey's electricity consumption, considering areas that are not yet organised industrial areas. In particular, OSB companies in Anatolia have an independent status in many areas until they reach their own electricity, water, natural gas, malfunction maintenance, zoning and construction licenses. In any case, it is not possible for industrial institutions to invest without this independent position. However, even though it has such broad authority and responsibility, non-enhanced organisations cannot be expected to do all this work without problems. While many organised industrial sector managers are not equipped to implement the EPDK legislation and rules, some have faced serious sanctions and penalties due to inadequacies. It is not possible for the OSBs to do the work with hundreds of employees that power distribution companies have run for billions of dollars. At the same time, OSBs need to be covered by related distribution companies without bringing additional burdens to the industry, the high-budget jobs required for tariffs, infrastructure, plant investments. A large number of OSBs in the east are afraid of power outages every minute due to problems in collecting electricity bills they cut to the industrialists. The OSBs, which house our country's motor companies, need to be supported and encouraged by the state, not only by land allocation support, but also by tariff modeling. It is vital that the OSBs provide cheap electricity supply for the OSBs to set up their own electricity facilities, allowing the state to provide interest and tax reductions. Dear friends, even though the cheap electricity fuel in the electric market has been stalled recently, it is now slowly beginning to walk on stronger ground. However, industrial companies have to buy electricity from a 15 per cent more expensive rate when they want to buy this service from electricity wholesale companies and go back to distribution companies, not from distribution companies. Because of this issue, the industry's desire to buy cheap electricity from the market is blocked by distribution companies. However, it is important for free market conditions to get electricity wherever everyone wants to be. Dear friends, sheets are published in newspapers every day where the free consumer limit is lowered, but it's not really easy to get electricity from the free market. After last year's February crisis, TİASC conditions have made it so difficult that it is not possible to compete with electricity wholesale companies without production or imports. TEİAS is buying the amount of electricity he sells from a wholesaler who wants to sell electricity as cash and collateral. In any case, wholesale companies who are trying to make one-digit profits to sell electricity to our industrialists for cheap are leaving this operation because of the collateral letter commission and high business capital. And the sad truth is that they will create a serious market pressure under the name of the service license, which has been separated from the distribution companies. This problem must be resolved, or we will remove our industrialists from the level that will compete with their opponents in the international market. With these feelings, I salute the entire General Assembly.

However, the problem here is to take HES dams within these renewable energy sources. So far, the HES numbers have exceeded 2,000, and the targeted HES dams are up to 10,000. We want to say, first of all, that we have repeatedly stated from this bench that the renewable energy source, we think, should be defined as taking energy in nature in its own flow and reusing it and using it again. When we look at it from this point of view, we see nothing that matches the description of the HESEs. To date, two things have been considered when judgment decisions were made concerning the HESEs; one is violation of the law; two is that there are irreparable damages. However, if the HES project has reached a certain point, if it has reached an irreversible point, it can continue on the basis of public benefit. Members of parliament, according to the second joke of Article 15 of the Civil Code, the President of the Grand National Assembly of Turkey decides which combinations the MPs will conduct. In this scope, the monthly presidential court's watch schedule includes clerks who will serve with the vice president for a certain week. According to this chart, when the mergers are conducted, the clerks on various obligations or needs can replace each other, and the vice presidents can also make a shift among themselves. In this framework, the Presidential Court can sometimes consist of members of the same political party in the face of an exceptional situation. For example: In the March 3, 2011 merger, Deputy President Nevzat Pakdil and Clerk members Bayram Özçelik and Harun Riflesçi are members. In the October 11, 2012, Deputy President Yakut and Clerk Members Mustafa Hamarat and Özlem Yemisçi were appointed in the Union. It's possible to replicate the samples. In this regard, on 18/11/2008, a formal debate was opened with the late Vice President Eyübüp Gülpınar and the Clerk Members Yusuf Çushkun and Fatma Salman. Second, it also uploads the cost of this audit to the controller. I mean, no matter how you look at it, it's a problem. Starting at the end, the fact that a public authority, a control authority, the costs of a mandate born from the implementation of a law, the costs of this control are being imposed on the audited sections, the audited sections that will be influenced by that law is not in line with our public tradition, nor is it in accordance with our legal system. Compensation for the consequences of implementing a law is one thing, but it is another thing to transfer the results of the implementation of a law into auditing its costs. For example, there's an zoning law arrangement, there's an infrastructure structure, you're taking it down, you're putting it back, and you're collecting the cost of it from the person who did it, that's a separate thing, but there's a different thing, for example, when you're trying to take the wages of those people who are responsible for implementing a legal arrangement of the Immmmmmmage Law, for example, from that controller. So, it's not in our legal system, and it's not in our tradition, so we strongly oppose it. However, the main challenge in this law is the transfer of public service, even pure public service, to the private sector. Now, the private sector has a very new location in the electricity market, especially in the energy market. So, we can't say he's very experienced, we can't say he's got a lot of serious information. This area, in fact, is an area where the public exists, where they've been around for years, where they've collected experience and experience. So, if we think that the private sector is not too much information and savings in this regard, we feel it is not only appropriate for them to be authorized to oversee and arrange reports about it, but we also think that it may lead to some abuse. More importantly, this arrangement is contrary to the Constitution. In fact, Law No. 5346 was annulled by the Constitutional Court in July 2012. Look, members of the Constitutional Court will be issued by the EPDK to buy services from the audit companies authorized by the EPDK, if necessary. Now, the Constitutional Court says it's against the Constitution. So the Constitutional Court finds it unconstitutional that a public service should be taken from the public and given control authority over the implementation of a law to the private sector. And yet, the matter that we're talking about depends on it, and it carries judgment in this same framework. Thus, here the breach of the Constitution is very clear and clear. Therefore, it is either disrespectful to the Constitutional Court to renegotiate and renegotiate a verdict that the Constitutional Court has cancelled in less than six months, and to accept the Constitution as if it does not exist, it is either disrespectful to the constitutional order, it is disrespectful to the framework established by the Constitution, or it has been prepared in a careless way. I mean, I don't think the people who made the ruling here, the ones who did an evaluation in this frame, could see it. In this regard, these provisions of the law are contrary to the Constitution, against Turkey's customs, against the legal system. Thus, these substances must be removed from the text of the law.
